\section{Higher-degree simple-Parry causal obstructions}
\label{sec:simple-parry-causal-obstructions}

The equalities between bounded-multiple order and optimal causal inverse
length proved in Theorem~\ref{thm:quadratic-optimal-causal-inverse} do not
extend universally beyond the stated quadratic cases.  We now identify the
finite object that governs
causal inversion in arbitrary simple-Parry rank folds and exhibit
higher-degree counterexamples in both directions.  This section continues to
concern the cyclic language rank of this article, not equality of numerical
$\beta$-values.

Recall that Pisot bases have eventually periodic greedy expansions of one
\cite{Schmidt1980}, whereas the finite expansion required below is the
simple-Parry hypothesis.  The collision results through
Theorem~\ref{thm:simple-parry-aperture-two} do not require the base to be
Pisot.  Parry admissibility and the associated Bertrand numeration are standard
\cite{Parry1960,CharlierCisterninoStipulanti2022}; the expansions of one for
cubic Pisot numbers were classified by Bassino \cite{Bassino2002}.  We record
the data needed
to distinguish these published inputs from the new sliding-congruence
statement.

Let $\beta\notin\mathbb Z$ be a simple-Parry number with
\[
d_\beta(1)=t_1t_2\cdots t_p0^\infty,
\qquad t_1=d=\lfloor\beta\rfloor,
\qquad t_p>0,
\]
where $p\ge2$ and $0\le t_i\le d$.  Put
\[
P_\beta(z)=z^p-t_1z^{p-1}-\cdots-t_p,
\qquad
\mathcal A_d=\{0,1,\ldots,d\},
\qquad
\Delta_d=[-d,d]\cap\mathbb Z.
\]
The raw system is the full shift $\mathcal A_d^{\mathbb Z}$, so $\Delta_d$
is exactly its digit-difference alphabet.  Let $Q_n$ be the number of legal
length-$n$ words.  In the low-to-high convention used throughout this
article, the established Parry--Bertrand rank data are
\begin{align}
Q_0&=1,\nonumber\\
Q_n&=1+\sum_{i=1}^n t_iQ_{n-i} &&(1\le n<p),\label{eq:simple-parry-Q}\\
Q_n&=\sum_{i=1}^p t_iQ_{n-i} &&(n\ge p),\nonumber
\end{align}
and
\[
\Rank_{\beta,m}(w)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}w_jQ_j
\]
maps the legal length-$m$ words, in colexicographic order, bijectively onto
$\{0,\ldots,Q_m-1\}$.  We therefore define $\Fold_{\beta,m}$ and
$\Phi_{\beta,m}$ by the same rank reduction modulo $Q_m$ as in
Definition~\ref{def:quadratic-parry-fold}.

For $m\ge2$, define
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)
=\min\{L\ge1:a_t=\psi(y_t,\ldots,y_{t+L-1})
\text{ for every }y=\Phi_{\beta,m}(a)\},
\label{eq:simple-parry-causal-length}
\end{equation}
with value $\infty$ if no such $L$ exists.  Also set
\[
\mathfrak a(P_\beta,d)
=\min\{\deg H:0\ne H\in(P_\beta),\ H(0)\ne0,
\ |[z^j]H|\le d\text{ for all }j\}.
\]

\begin{proposition}[Degeneration of the bounded-multiple order]
\label{prop:simple-parry-bounded-order}
Under the preceding simple-Parry hypotheses,
\[
\mathfrak a(P_\beta,d)=p.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The coefficient vector of $P_\beta$ consists of the leading coefficient
$1$ and the coefficients $-t_1,\ldots,-t_p$.  It lies in $\Delta_d$, and its
constant coefficient $-t_p$ is nonzero.  Hence
$\mathfrak a(P_\beta,d)\le p$.  Conversely, every nonzero member of the
principal ideal $(P_\beta)\subset\mathbb Z[z]$ has degree at least
$\deg P_\beta=p$.  This proves equality.
\end{proof}

For $L\ge1$, introduce the bounded sliding-congruence obstruction
\begin{equation}
\mathcal N_{\beta,m,L}
=\left\{e\in\Delta_d^{m+L-1}:
e_0\ne0,\quad
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_je_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}
\ (0\le t<L)\right\}.
\label{eq:bad-sliding-congruence-set}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, let $T_{m,L}$ be the $L$-row banded Toeplitz matrix obtained by
placing the kernel $(Q_0,\ldots,Q_{m-1})$ at offsets
$t=0,\ldots,L-1$.  Then the last condition is
$T_{m,L}e\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}$.

\begin{theorem}[Exact causal collision criterion]
\label{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision}
For every nonintegral simple-Parry number as above and every $m\ge2$,
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)
=\min\{L\ge1:\mathcal N_{\beta,m,L}=\varnothing\},
\label{eq:exact-causal-obstruction}
\end{equation}
where the minimum is $\infty$ when all the obstruction sets are nonempty.

More explicitly, let $\Gamma_{\beta,m}$ be the directed graph with vertex set
$V=\Delta_d^{m-1}$.  There is an edge from
$v=(v_0,\ldots,v_{m-2})$ to $w=(w_0,\ldots,w_{m-2})$ precisely when
\[
w_i=v_{i+1}\quad(0\le i<m-2)
\]
and
\begin{equation}
\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}Q_jv_j+Q_{m-1}w_{m-2}
\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}.
\label{eq:collision-graph-edge}
\end{equation}
If $B=\{v\in V:v_0\ne0\}$, then
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)
=1+\sup\{\text{number of edges in a directed path starting in }B\}.
\label{eq:collision-graph-longest-path}
\end{equation}
The supremum is infinite exactly when a directed cycle is reachable from
$B$.  Furthermore,
\begin{equation}
\Phi_{\beta,m}\text{ is injective}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Gamma_{\beta,m}\text{ has no bi-infinite path other than the zero path}.
\label{eq:collision-graph-injectivity}
\end{equation}
If $\Phi_{\beta,m}$ is injective, then its causal inverse is finite exactly
when the zero vertex is not reachable from $B$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $u,v\in\mathcal A_d^{m+L-1}$ and put $e_i=u_i-v_i$.  Since the legal
rank is a bijection, the $t$th output symbols of the two blocks are equal if
and only if their raw ranks agree modulo $Q_m$, namely if and only if
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_je_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}.
\]
Moreover, $u_0\ne v_0$ if and only if $e_0\ne0$.  Thus every pair of raw
blocks whose first symbols remain ambiguous after $L$ outputs determines an
element of $\mathcal N_{\beta,m,L}$.

Conversely, for any $e\in\Delta_d^{m+L-1}$ define coordinatewise
\[
u_i=\max\{e_i,0\},\qquad v_i=\max\{-e_i,0\}.
\]
Then $u_i,v_i\in\mathcal A_d$, $u_i-v_i=e_i$, and both finite blocks extend
arbitrarily to points of the full shift.  Hence every element of
$\mathcal N_{\beta,m,L}$ is realized by an actual output collision with
distinct first symbols.  If the obstruction set is empty, the first raw
symbol is constant on every fiber over an occurring length-$L$ output word;
the resulting function on this finite output language is the decoder $\psi$
in \eqref{eq:simple-parry-causal-length}.  This proves
\eqref{eq:exact-causal-obstruction}.

A length-$L$ path in $\Gamma_{\beta,m}$ beginning at
$(e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\in B$ is exactly a vector
$e\in\mathcal N_{\beta,m,L}$: each edge appends one coordinate and imposes
one of the $L$ congruences.  Formula
\eqref{eq:collision-graph-longest-path} follows.  Since the graph is finite,
arbitrarily long such paths exist exactly when a directed cycle is reachable
from $B$.

A bi-infinite difference sequence labels a bi-infinite path in the graph if
and only if all corresponding output ranks agree.  Every coordinate of that
difference sequence is realized by two raw digits by the positive--negative
part construction above.  Therefore a nonzero bi-infinite graph path is
equivalent to a pair of distinct points with the same image, proving
\eqref{eq:collision-graph-injectivity}.

Finally assume injectivity.  No directed cycle containing a nonzero vertex
can then occur, because its periodic repetition would be a nonzero
bi-infinite path.  The zero loop is consequently the only cycle reachable
from $B$ that is compatible with injectivity.  Thus paths from $B$ have
unbounded length exactly when they reach the zero vertex.  Combining this
with \eqref{eq:collision-graph-longest-path} proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Causal completeness and periodic detection]
\label{cor:simple-parry-causal-completeness}
Under the preceding hypotheses, for every $m\ge2$ the following are

 succeeded in 238ms:
\beta^4 g(2/\beta)
=4(3+4\beta-\beta^3)>0,
\]
where the last inequality follows from
$\beta<11/5$ and
$3+4\beta-\beta^3>11-(11/5)^3=44/125$.
Thus $u<2/\beta$.  The product of the four roots is $-2$, whence
\[
|z|^2=\frac{2}{\beta u}>1.
\]
Irreducibility makes $z$ a Galois conjugate of $\beta$, so $\beta$ is not
Pisot.  Finally $d=2$, $p=4$, and
$c_\beta=t_2+1=1<d$; case~\textup{(b)} of
Theorem~\ref{thm:simple-parry-aperture-two} yields injectivity and
$\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,2)=2$.
\end{proof}

The graph in Theorem~\ref{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision} is the arithmetic
specialization of the standard fiber-product collision graph for a sliding
block code; see \cite{LindMarcus1995,Nasu1995}.  Its substantive role here is
that it separates the exact modulus-$Q_m$ survival depth from the unrelated
degree of a bounded polynomial multiple.

We next show that, even in algebraic degree three, the causal lengths at
injective apertures have no uniform bound.  Bassino's classification
\cite{Bassino2002} contains the cubic Pisot family and its finite expansion
of one used below.  We include a direct verification of those input data so
that the new part of the argument is visibly confined to the sliding
collision depth.

For $n\ge4$, put
\begin{equation}
M_n(x)=x^3-(n+2)x^2+2nx-n
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-polynomial}
\end{equation}
and define the word
\begin{equation}
w_n=n\,1\,2\cdots(n-2)\,n\,1\,0\,
(n-1)(n-2)\cdots2\,0\,n.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-parry-word}
\end{equation}
Thus $|w_n|=2n+2$.

\begin{lemma}[Cubic family data]
\label{lem:cubic-unbounded-family-data}
The polynomial $M_n$ has a unique real root $\beta_n$, this root belongs to
$(n,n+1)$, and $\beta_n$ is a nonintegral cubic Pisot number.  Moreover,
\[
d_{\beta_n}(1)=w_n0^\infty.
\]
If $(Q_j)$ denotes its Parry--Bertrand language counts, then, for
$0\le r\le n-1$,
\begin{align}
Q_0&=1,\qquad Q_1=n+1,\qquad Q_2=n^2+n+2,
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-initial-Q}\\
Q_r&=(n+2)Q_{r-1}-2nQ_{r-2}+nQ_{r-3}
\qquad(3\le r\le n-1).
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-Q-recurrence}
\end{align}
In particular $Q_{j+1}>nQ_j$ throughout this range.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
One has
\[
M_n(n)=-n<0,
\qquad
M_n(n+1)=n^2-n-1>0.
\]
The identity
\[
M_n(x)=x^2(x-2)-n(x-1)^2
\]
shows that $M_n(x)<0$ for $x\le n$.  Indeed this is immediate for
$x\le2$, while for $2<x\le n$ one uses
\[
\frac{x^2(x-2)}{(x-1)^2}
=x-\frac{x}{(x-1)^2}<x\le n.
\]
If all three roots were real, every root other than the one in $(n,n+1)$
would also have to exceed $n$; their sum could then not equal $n+2$.
Consequently $\beta_n$ is the unique real root.  A reducible monic cubic
over $\mathbb Z$ has an integral root, which is impossible because the only
real root lies strictly between consecutive integers.  Thus $M_n$ is
irreducible.  Its two remaining roots are conjugate, and their common
modulus is
\[
\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_n}}<1
\]
by the product of the roots.  This proves the asserted Pisot and degree
statements.

Let $P_{w_n}(x)=x^{2n+2}-\sum_{i=1}^{2n+2}(w_n)_i
x^{2n+2-i}$ be the Parry polynomial of the displayed word.  Direct
multiplication gives the polynomial identity
\begin{equation}
P_{w_n}(x)
=M_n(x)\frac{(x^n-1)(x^{n+1}-1)}{(x-1)^2}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-parry-factorization}
\end{equation}
Hence $w_n$ represents one in base $\beta_n$.  The digit $n$ occurs only
at the first, the $n$th, and the last positions.  The suffix beginning at
the $n$th position starts with $n10$, whereas $w_n$ starts with $n12$;
the last suffix is $n0^\infty$.  Every other proper suffix starts with a
digit below $n$.  All proper suffixes are therefore lexicographically
smaller than $w_n0^\infty$, so Parry's criterion gives the claimed greedy
expansion.

For $r\le n-1$, its first $r$ digits are $t_1=n$ and $t_i=i-1$ for
$2\le i\le r$.  Formula~\eqref{eq:simple-parry-Q} becomes
\begin{equation}
Q_r=1+nQ_{r-1}+\sum_{i=2}^r(i-1)Q_{r-i}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-positive-Q}
\end{equation}
This gives \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-initial-Q}, proves $Q_r>nQ_{r-1}$,
and, by subtracting the formula at the preceding three indices, gives
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-Q-recurrence}.
\end{proof}

For the next lemma write $S_k=\sum_{j=0}^kQ_j$ and
$D_k=Q_k-nQ_{k-1}$.  Formula
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-positive-Q} and the cubic recurrence give,
respectively,
\begin{align}
D_k&=1+\sum_{i=2}^k(i-1)Q_{k-i},
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-positive}\\
D_k&=2Q_{k-2}-(n-2)Q_{k-3}+2S_{k-4}
\qquad(k\ge3),
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-alternate}
\end{align}
where $S_{-1}=0$.

\begin{lemma}[Parameter-uniform two-window carry exclusion]
\label{lem:cubic-unbounded-two-window}
Let $n\ge4$ and $2\le r\le n-1$.  If
$e_0,\ldots,e_r\in[-n,n]\cap\mathbb Z$ and
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}Q_je_j=c_0Q_r,
\qquad
\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}Q_je_{j+1}=c_1Q_r
\]
for integers $c_0,c_1$, then $c_0,c_1\in\{-1,0,1\}$ and $c_1=0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since $Q_{j+1}>nQ_j$, geometric summation gives
\begin{equation}
S_k<\frac{n}{n-1}Q_k.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-geometric-bound}
\end{equation}
It follows that $nS_{r-1}<2Q_r$, so every carry is in
$\{-1,0,1\}$.  We also record the possible last digit of one window.  If
the carry is zero, \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-geometric-bound} gives
$nS_{r-2}<2Q_{r-1}$, so the last digit belongs to $\{-1,0,1\}$.
For a carry equal to one, the last digit is $n$.  To see this, note first
that, for $r\ge3$,
\[
D_r+D_{r-1}>Q_{r-2}+3Q_{r-3}>(n+3)Q_{r-3}
>nS_{r-3}.
\]
Together with $Q_{r-1}=nQ_{r-2}+D_{r-1}$, this says
\begin{equation}
Q_r-(n-1)Q_{r-1}>nS_{r-2}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-last-digit-gap}
\end{equation}
The case $r=2$ follows directly from
$Q_2-(n-1)Q_1=n+3>n$.  Thus a last digit at most $n-1$ cannot produce
the sum $Q_r$.  Sign symmetry gives last digit $-n$ for carry $-1$.

Suppose now, toward a contradiction, that $c_1=1$; the negative case is
obtained by changing all signs.  Put $f_j=e_{j+1}$ for
$0\le j\le r-2$.  The last-digit conclusion gives $e_r=n$, and hence
\begin{equation}
A:=\sum_{j=0}^{r-2}Q_jf_j=D_r.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-prefix-D}
\end{equation}
Set $B=\sum_{j=0}^{r-2}Q_{j+1}f_j$.  We shall prove
\begin{equation}
n<B<Q_r-n.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-shifted-gap}
\end{equation}
This is already explicit at the two small apertures.  If $r=2$, then
$f_0=2$ and $B=2(n+1)$.  If $r=3$, equation
$f_0+(n+1)f_1=n+4$ and the digit bounds give
\[
(f_0,f_1)=(3,1)\quad\hbox{or}\quad(2-n,2).
\]
The corresponding values of $B$ are $n^2+4n+5$ and $n^2+3n+6$.
They satisfy \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-shifted-gap} for $n\ge4$.

Assume $r\ge4$.  Let $\alpha,\overline\alpha$ be the nonreal conjugates
of $\beta=\beta_n$, and put
\[
s=\alpha+\overline\alpha=n+2-\beta,
\qquad p=\alpha\overline\alpha=\frac n\beta.
\]
For $u_j=Q_{j+1}-\beta Q_j$, the cubic recurrence with its dominant
$\beta$ component removed gives
\[
u_{j+2}=su_{j+1}-pu_j.
\]
The quadratic quantity
\[
I_j=u_{j+1}^2-su_ju_{j+1}+pu_j^2
\]
satisfies $I_j=p^jI_0$.  The two exact completed-square forms are
\begin{align*}
I_j&=\left(u_{j+1}-\frac{s}{2}u_j\right)^2
 +\left(p-\frac{s^2}{4}\right)u_j^2,\\
I_j&=p\left(u_j-\frac{s}{2p}u_{j+1}\right)^2
 +\frac{p-s^2/4}{p}u_{j+1}^2.
\end{align*}
Since the conjugates are nonreal, $p-s^2/4>0$.  Finally, direct
substitution of $M_n(\beta)=0$ gives
\[
4\left(p-\frac{s^2}{4}\right)-I_0
=\frac{(\beta-n)(2\beta+n-4)}{\beta}>0.
\]
Consequently $|u_j|<2$ throughout the required range, and
\begin{equation}
|B-\beta D_r|
=\left|\sum_{j=0}^{r-2}u_jf_j\right|<2n(r-1).
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-conjugate-error}
\end{equation}

The positive formula for $D_r$ gives $D_r>Q_{r-2}\ge Q_2$.  Since
$r\le n-1$, the lower bound in
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-conjugate-error} is therefore larger than $n$.
For the upper bound, \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-geometric-bound} and
$Q_{r-3}>nQ_{r-4}$ give
\[
2S_{r-4}<\frac{2}{n-1}Q_{r-3}
\le(n-2)Q_{r-3}.
\]
Thus \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-alternate} yields
$D_r<2Q_{r-2}<Q_{r-1}/2$.  Using $\beta-1<n$,
\[
Q_r-\beta D_r
=nQ_{r-1}-(\beta-1)D_r
>\frac n2Q_{r-1}.
\]
As $Q_{r-1}\ge Q_3=n^3+n^2+3n+4$, subtracting the error
$2n(r-1)\le2n(n-2)$ still leaves more than $n$.  This proves
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-shifted-gap}.

The first window sum is $e_0+B$.  By
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-shifted-gap} and $|e_0|\le n$, it lies strictly
between $0$ and $Q_r$, contradicting its membership in $Q_r\mathbb Z$.
Therefore $c_1=0$.
\end{proof}

For $L\ge1$, let $\mathcal K_{n,r,L}$ be the set obtained from
$\mathcal N_{\beta_n,r,L}$ by allowing the first coordinate to vanish.
Thus it consists of all $e\in[-n,n]^{r+L-1}\cap\mathbb Z^{r+L-1}$ whose
$L$ sliding sums vanish modulo $Q_r$.  Define
\[
E_{n,2}=(2,n),
\qquad
E_{n,3}=(n-2,-2,-n,1),
\qquad
E_{n,r+1}=(-2,E_{n,r},0)\quad(r\ge3).
\]

\begin{lemma}[Exact terminal collision paths]
\label{lem:cubic-unbounded-terminal-paths}
For every $n\ge4$ and $2\le r\le n-1$,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal K_{n,r,r-1}=\{0,\ E_{n,r},\ -E_{n,r}\},
\qquad
\mathcal K_{n,r,r}=\{0\}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-terminal-classification}
\end{equation}
For $r\ge3$, the exact window sums of $E_{n,r}$ are
$-Q_r,0,\ldots,0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The identity \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-alternate} is equivalently
\begin{equation}
-2\sum_{j=0}^{k-4}Q_j+(n-2)Q_{k-3}-2Q_{k-2}
-nQ_{k-1}+Q_k=0
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-zero-word}
\end{equation}
for $k\ge3$; for $k=2$ the corresponding identity is
$-2-nQ_1+Q_2=0$.  Sliding these identities through the displayed vector
$E_{n,r}$ proves directly that its window sums are $-Q_r,0,\ldots,0$.
The vector $E_{n,2}$ has its sole sum equal to $Q_2$.

We prove the classification by induction on $r$.  At $r=2$, the bound
$|e_0+(n+1)e_1|<2Q_2$ shows
\[
\mathcal K_{n,2,1}=\{(0,0),\ \pm(2,n)\}.
\]
For two windows, Lemma~\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-two-window} makes the
second carry zero.  Then $e_1+(n+1)e_2=0$ forces $e_1=e_2=0$, and the
first congruence forces $e_0=0$.  Hence
$\mathcal K_{n,2,2}=\{0\}$.

The case $r=3$ can also be read without any omitted branch.  In a
two-window path the second carry is zero, so
\[
e_1+(n+1)e_2+Q_2e_3=0.
\]
The last-digit bound in the preceding lemma gives
$e_3\in\{-1,0,1\}$.  If $e_3=0$, division by $Q_1=n+1>n$ gives
$e_1=e_2=0$; if $e_3=1$, it gives $(e_1,e_2)=(-2,-n)$; the remaining
case is its negative.  The first congruence then determines
$e_0=n-2$, $0$, or $2-n$, respectively.  Thus
$\mathcal K_{n,3,2}=\{0,\pm E_{n,3}\}$.  Applying the already proved
$r=2$ two-window classification to the last two zero-carry windows gives
$\mathcal K_{n,3,3}=\{0\}$.

Now let $r\ge4$ and assume both assertions at aperture $r-1$.  Take
$e\in\mathcal K_{n,r,r-1}$, and write its window sums as $c_tQ_r$.
Repeated application of Lemma~\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-two-window} gives
$c_1=\cdots=c_{r-2}=0$.  Drop $e_0$ and the final coordinate of $e$.
For $0\le t\le r-3$, the remaining length-$(r-1)$ window sum is
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{r-2}Q_je_{t+1+j}
=-Q_{r-1}e_{t+r}.
\]
Hence the resulting vector belongs to
$\mathcal K_{n,r-1,r-2}$ and is, by induction, $0$ or
$\pm E_{n,r-1}$.  In the zero case the last zero-carry equation and then
the first congruence force the two omitted coordinates to vanish.  In the
nonzero cases the exact window sums already established force the final
coordinate to be zero; the first congruence then forces the initial
coordinate to be $-2$ or $2$.  These are precisely
$E_{n,r}=(-2,E_{n,r-1},0)$ and its negative.  This proves the first
classification.

Finally take $e\in\mathcal K_{n,r,r}$.  The last $r-1$ carries are zero.
Dropping the first and final coordinates now produces an element of
$\mathcal K_{n,r-1,r-1}$, which is zero by induction.  The last equation
then kills the final coordinate, and the first equation kills the initial
one.  Thus $\mathcal K_{n,r,r}=\{0\}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Unbounded causal depth in algebraic degree three]
\label{thm:cubic-unbounded-causal-depth}
For every $n\ge4$, let $\beta_n\in(n,n+1)$ be the root of
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-polynomial} and put $m_n=n-1$.  Then $\beta_n$
is a nonintegral cubic simple-Parry Pisot number, $\Phi_{\beta_n,m_n}$ is
injective, and
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta_n,m_n)=n-1.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-causal-length}
\end{equation}
Consequently
\[
\sup\left\{\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m):
\substack{\beta\text{ nonintegral cubic simple-Parry Pisot},\\
m\ge2,\ \Phi_{\beta,m}\text{ injective}}\right\}=\infty.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-family-data} supplies all base and language
hypotheses.  At $r=m_n$, Lemma
\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-terminal-paths} gives
\[
\mathcal N_{\beta_n,r,r-1}=\{E_{n,r},-E_{n,r}\},
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\beta_n,r,r}=\varnothing.
\]
The exact collision criterion, Theorem
\ref{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision}, now gives
$\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta_n,m_n)=m_n=n-1$.  Its finiteness implies
injectivity by Corollary~\ref{cor:simple-parry-causal-completeness}, and
letting $n$ tend to infinity proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[An unbounded $p$-bonacci separation]
\label{cor:pbonacci-unbounded-separation}
For $p\ge3$, let $\beta_p$ be the $p$-bonacci number, the real root of
\[
P_p(z)=z^p-z^{p-1}-\cdots-z-1.
\]
At aperture $m=p+1$ the cyclic rank fold is injective and
\[

 succeeded in 237ms:
Thus, for $m\ge2$,
\[
R_{m-1}=Q_{m-1}-bQ_{m-2}
\]
and
\[
Q_m=dQ_{m-1}+R_{m-1}
=(d+1)Q_{m-1}-bQ_{m-2}
=aQ_{m-1}-bQ_{m-2},
\]
which proves \eqref{eq:qplus}.  The order of the displayed digit classes is
the colexicographic order, so the same induction proves order preservation.
When $b=1$ in the negative chamber, the rule and recurrence are precisely
those of Definition~\ref{def:metallic-window-data}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The bounded sliding-congruence lemma}

The separation estimate used in the sliding argument is isolated first.  In
particular, the integer multiple and the error term are both retained.

\begin{lemma}[Nearest-multiple separation]
\label{lem:nearest-multiple-separation}
Let $Q_0,Q_1,\ldots$ satisfy either \eqref{eq:qminus} or \eqref{eq:qplus},
write $s=1$ or $s=-1$ accordingly, and put
\[
d=\begin{cases}a,&s=1,\\a-1,&s=-1.\end{cases}
\]
Then, for $r\ge4$ and $1\le |e|\le d$,
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{dist}(beQ_{r-1},Q_r\mathbb Z)\ge Q_{r-2}.
\label{eq:quadratic-separation}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Distance is unchanged by replacing $e$ with $-e$, so assume $1\le e\le d$.
Set
\[
R_r:=\frac{Q_{r-1}}{Q_{r-2}}.
\]
The recurrence gives $R_{r+1}=a+sb/R_r$.  Its initial values and the parameter
ranges imply, by induction,
\begin{align}
a+\frac{b}{a+1}&\le R_r<a+\frac ba &&(s=1,\ r\ge3),
\label{eq:ratio-plus}\\
a-\frac{b}{a-1}&<R_r\le a-\frac ba &&(s=-1,\ r\ge3).
\label{eq:ratio-minus}
\end{align}
For completeness, in the positive-recurrence case the map
$x\mapsto a+b/x$ reverses order, sends
$[a+b/(a+1),a+b/a)$ into itself, and $R_3=a+b/(a+1)$.
In the negative-recurrence case $x\mapsto a-b/x$ preserves order, the interval
in \eqref{eq:ratio-minus} lies in $(a-1,a)$, and $R_3=a-b/a$.
These observations prove the two inductions, including the stated strict
endpoints.

We shall also use the following sharpened bounds, obtained by applying the
same recurrence once more:
\begin{align*}
L_+&:=a+\frac{b}{a+b/(a+b/(a+1))},&
U_+&:=a+\frac{b}{a+b/(a+1)},\\
L_-&:=a-\frac{b}{a-1},&
U_-&:=a-\frac{b}{a-b/a}.
\end{align*}
Thus
\begin{equation}
L_+\le R_r\le U_+\quad(s=1,r\ge4),
\qquad
L_-<R_r\le U_-\quad(s=-1,r\ge4).
\label{eq:sharpened-ratio-bounds}
\end{equation}
The first assertion follows because the order-reversing map interchanges the
endpoints $R_4=U_+$ and $R_5=L_+$ and maps $[L_+,U_+]$ into itself.  The
second follows because $R_3=a-b/a$, $R_4=U_-$, and the increasing map sends
$(L_-,U_-]$ into itself.

Choose a nearest integer $k$ to
\[
z:=\frac{beQ_{r-1}}{Q_r}=\frac{be}{R_{r+1}}.
\]
The lower bounds $R_{r+1}>a$ for $s=1$ and $R_{r+1}>a-1$ for $s=-1$ give
\[
0<z<b.
\]
Indeed $e\le a$ in the first case and $e\le a-1$ in the second.  Hence every
nearest integer satisfies
\begin{equation}
0\le k\le b.
\label{eq:nearest-k-bound}
\end{equation}
Put $h:=be-ak$.  The exact error, with no omitted remainder, is
\begin{align}
E&:=beQ_{r-1}-kQ_r\notag\\
 &=(be-ak)Q_{r-1}-skbQ_{r-2}
   =(hR_r-skb)Q_{r-2}.
\label{eq:nearest-error}
\end{align}

We first record the bounded integer exclusion for the cancelling sign in the
positive recurrence.  For $1\le e\le a$, $0\le k\le b$, and $h=be-ak$,
\begin{equation}
h>0\Longrightarrow
  hL_+-kb\ge1\quad\hbox{or}\quad kb-hU_+\ge1.
\label{eq:endpoint-plus}
\end{equation}
Write $j=kb-ah$.  Since $h=be-ak$, solving for $(e,k)$ gives
\[
k=\frac{ah+j}{b},\qquad
e=\frac{(a^2+b)h+aj}{b^2}.
\]
Thus the two displayed numerators are divisible by $b$ and $b^2$,
respectively.  We give the remaining Euclidean divisions explicitly.  Write
\[
be=aq+r,\qquad qb=ap+t,\qquad 0\le r,t<a.
\]
If $k\le q-1$, then $h\ge a+r$ and
\[
hL_+-kb>(a+r)a-(b-1)b\ge1.
\]
If $k\ge q+1$, then $h<0$, so this case is not in the hypothesis of
\eqref{eq:endpoint-plus}.  Hence only $k=q$ remains, and then $h=r$.
Now $j=a(p-r)+t$.  The cases $p<r$ and $p>r$ give, respectively,
$hL_+-kb\ge1$ and $kb-hU_+\ge1$.  Explicitly, when $p<r$,
\[
rL_+-qb=a(r-p)-t+r(L_+-a)>a-t\ge1,
\]
whereas, when $p>r$, the bounds $0<U_+-a<b/a$ and $r<a$ give
\[
qb-rU_+=a(p-r)+t-r(U_+-a)>a-\frac{rb}{a}\ge1.
\]
When $p=r$, put $w=rb-at$.  The two division identities give
\begin{equation}
b^2e=r(a^2+b)+at,\qquad w=r(a^2+2b)-b^2e.
\label{eq:plus-terminal-divisions}
\end{equation}
The integer $w$ is nonzero.  To verify this without a hidden coprimality
assumption, suppose $w=0$ and write $a=gA$, $b=gB$ with
$\gcd(A,B)=1$.  From $at=rb$ one obtains $r=AR$ and $t=BR$ with
$0<R<g$.  Reducing $be=aq+r$ and $qb=ar+t$ modulo $g$ gives
$g\mid AR$ and $g\mid BR$.  Bezout's identity gives $g\mid R$, a
contradiction.  Substitution of
\eqref{eq:plus-terminal-divisions}, followed by multiplication by the positive
denominators, gives the exhaustive terminal alternatives
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
w>0& rb(a^2+a+b)\ge(t+1)(a^3+a^2+2ab+b),\\
w<0& t(a^2+a+b)\ge rb(a+1)+(a^2+a+b).
\end{array}
\]
There is no ratio estimate hidden in this table.  Indeed,
\[
U_+-a=\frac{b(a+1)}{a^2+a+b},\qquad
L_+-a=\frac{b(a^2+a+b)}{a^3+a^2+2ab+b}.
\]
Thus the first row is exactly $r(L_+-a)-t\ge1$, after multiplication by
its positive denominator, and the second is exactly
$t-r(U_+-a)\ge1$.  The signs are exhaustive because $w\ne0$; the two
identities in \eqref{eq:plus-terminal-divisions} retain every quotient and
remainder used in clearing the denominators.  This proves
\eqref{eq:endpoint-plus}.

The cancelling sign for the negative recurrence has a shorter proof which
avoids Euclidean endpoint bookkeeping.  Let $\beta$ and $\alpha$ be the
roots of $x^2-ax+b$, with $0<\alpha<1<\beta$, and put
$g=-h=ak-be>0$.  The closed formula for the recurrence gives
\[
R_r=\frac{\beta^r-\alpha^r}{\beta^{r-1}-\alpha^{r-1}}>\beta,
\]
while \eqref{eq:sharpened-ratio-bounds} gives $R_r\le U_-$.  Consider the
nonzero integral norm
\[
\nu:=k^2-aek+be^2=(k-e\alpha)(k-e\beta).
\]
Here $k\le b\le a-2<\beta\le e\beta$, so the second factor is negative.

If $\nu<0$, then $k-e\alpha>0$, and $b=\alpha\beta$ and
$\beta^2=a\beta-b$ give
\[
g\beta-kb=\beta^2(k-e\alpha)
=\frac{\beta^2|\nu|}{e\beta-k}>1;
\]
the last inequality uses $e\le a-1<\beta$.  Hence $gR_r-kb>1$.

Suppose $\nu>0$ and set $\delta=e\alpha-k>0$.  Then
\[
g=e\alpha^2-a\delta>0,
\qquad
\nu=\delta\{e(\beta-\alpha)+\delta\}\ge1.
\]
In particular
$0<\delta<e\alpha^2/a\le(a-1)\alpha^2/a<1$.  Put $D=a^2-b$ and
$H=(a-1)(\beta-\alpha)+1$.  Since $e\le a-1$, the norm identity gives
$\delta>H^{-1}$.  Moreover
\[
\alpha-\frac{ab}{D}=\frac{\alpha^3}{D},
\]
and therefore
\begin{align*}
kb-gU_-
&=\beta^2\delta-\frac{g\alpha^3}{D}\\
&=\delta\left(\beta^2+\frac{a\alpha^3}{D}\right)
  -\frac{e\alpha^5}{D}\\
&>\frac{\beta^2}{H}-\frac{(a-1)\alpha^5}{D}>1.
\end{align*}
For the final inequality, all margins are displayed:
\[
D-H=2\alpha^2+\alpha(\beta-1)+(\beta-1)>0
\]
and
\[
\beta^2-H=\beta-1-\alpha(1-\alpha)>(a-1)\alpha^5.
\]
Indeed $\alpha^5<\alpha$, while $b\le a-2$ is equivalent to
$\beta(1-\alpha)\ge2-\alpha>1$.  Thus
$(\beta^2-H)/H>(a-1)\alpha^5/D$.  Since $R_r\le U_-$, this case gives
$kb-gR_r>1$.

We now cover all signs explicitly.  If $s=1$ and $h<0$, then
\[
|hR_r-kb|=(-h)R_r+kb>1.
\]
If $h=0$, then $k\ge1$ (because $e\ge1$), and
$|hR_r-kb|=kb\ge1$.  If $h>0$, the two alternatives in
\eqref{eq:endpoint-plus}, together with
$L_+\le R_r\le U_+$, give $|hR_r-kb|\ge1$.

If $s=-1$ and $h>0$, then $hR_r+kb>1$.  If $h=0$, again $k\ge1$ and the
modulus is $kb\ge1$.  The case $h<0$ was proved directly above.
Thus in both chambers
\[
|hR_r-skb|\ge1.
\]
Equation~\eqref{eq:nearest-error} now yields $|E|\ge Q_{r-2}$, which is
\eqref{eq:quadratic-separation}.
\end{proof}

The following sliding lemma is the arithmetic core of the block decoder.

\begin{lemma}[Quadratic bounded sliding congruences]
\label{lem:quadratic-sliding-congruences}
Take either parameter chamber in
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-pisot-chambers}, let $d=\lfloor\beta\rfloor$, and
let $(Q_j)$ be \eqref{eq:qminus} or \eqref{eq:qplus}.  If $m\ge3$ and
$e_0,\ldots,e_{2m-2}\in[-d,d]\cap\mathbb Z$ satisfy
\begin{equation}
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_j e_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}
\qquad(0\le t<m),\label{eq:sliding-kernel}
\end{equation}
then $e_0=\cdots=e_{2m-2}=0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $s=1$ in the negative chamber and $s=-1$ in the positive chamber, so
$Q_{j+2}=aQ_{j+1}+sbQ_j$, and put
$\delta=Q_1-a$; hence $(d,\delta)=(a,1)$ for $s=1$ and
$(d,\delta)=(a-1,0)$ for $s=-1$.  Subtracting $a$ times the congruence at
$t+1$ and $sb$ times that at $t+2$ from the congruence at $t$ cancels every
interior coefficient and gives
\begin{equation}
e_t+\delta e_{t+1}-sbQ_{m-1}e_{t+m+1}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}.
\label{eq:quadratic-elimination}
\end{equation}

Suppose first that $m\ge4$.  The small part of
\eqref{eq:quadratic-elimination} has modulus at most
$d(1+\delta)$.  On the other hand,
\[
Q_{m-2}\ge Q_2=
\begin{cases}
a^2+a+b>2a=d(1+\delta),&s=1,\\
a^2-b>a-1=d(1+\delta),&s=-1.
\end{cases}
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:nearest-multiple-separation} therefore gives a strictly larger
distance whenever $e_{t+m+1}\ne0$.  Hence
$e_{t+m+1}=0$ for $0\le t\le m-3$.  Thus
$e_{m+1}=\cdots=e_{2m-2}=0$.  The final original congruence reduces to
\[
e_{m-1}+Q_1e_m\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}.
\]
Its left side has modulus at most $d(1+Q_1)<Q_m$ and $Q_1>d$, so both
digits vanish.  Back substitution in the preceding congruences then gives
$e_{m-2},\ldots,e_0=0$ successively.

It remains to check $m=3$, where endpoint equality in the separation bound
can occur.  Here each integer sum in \eqref{eq:sliding-kernel} has modulus
less than $2Q_3$, so it is $k_tQ_3$ with $k_t\in\{-1,0,1\}$.  Substituting
the three values of $t$ gives
\begin{equation}
e_t+Q_1e_{t+1}+Q_2e_{t+2}=k_tQ_3,
\qquad t=0,1,2.
\label{eq:quadratic-m3-equalities}
\end{equation}
We now eliminate the two conjugate signs separately.

Suppose first that $s=1$.  Thus $d=a$ and
\[
(Q_1,Q_2,Q_3)
=(a+1,a^2+a+b,a^3+a^2+2ab+b).
\]
Consider one equation $x+Q_1y+Q_2z=kQ_3$ occurring in
\eqref{eq:quadratic-m3-equalities}.  By symmetry it suffices to treat
$k=0,1$.  If $k=0$, then $|z|\ge2$ is impossible because
$2Q_2>a(1+Q_1)$.  The choice $z=0$ gives $x=y=0$, while $z=1$ gives
\[
x+b+(a+1)(y+a)=0.
\]
Since $y+a\ge0$ and $-(x+b)\le a-b<a+1$, this forces
$(x,y,z)=(-b,-a,1)$; the case $z=-1$ is its negative.  If $k=1$ and
$z\le a-1$, then
\[
x+Q_1y+Q_2z
\le a+aQ_1+(a-1)Q_2
=Q_3-\bigl(b(a+2)-a\bigr)<Q_3.
\]
Hence $z=a$, after which $x+(a+1)y=b(a+1)$ forces $(x,y)=(0,b)$.
Consequently every bounded solution of one equation is
\[
(x,y,z;k)\in
\{(0,0,0;0),\ \pm(b,a,-1;0),\ \pm(0,b,a;1)\}.
\]
The terminal pairs of these triples are
$(0,0)$, $\pm(a,-1)$, and $\pm(b,a)$.  If $e_4\ne0$ and the $t=2$
triple is $\pm(0,b,a)$, its initial pair $\pm(0,b)$ cannot be a terminal
pair of the $t=1$ triple.  If instead the $t=2$ triple is
$\pm(b,a,-1)$, the overlap forces the $t=1$ triple to be
$\pm(0,b,a)$, whose initial pair $\pm(0,b)$ cannot be a terminal pair of
the $t=0$ triple.  Both alternatives are impossible, and therefore
$e_4=0$.

Suppose next that $s=-1$.  Put $C=a-b$ and $d=a-1$; then
$2\le C\le d$ and
\[
(Q_1,Q_2,Q_3)=(a,a^2-b,a^3-2ab).
\]
For one equation $x+ay+Q_2z=kQ_3$, again it suffices to take $k=0,1$.
When $k=0$, the inequality $2Q_2>d(1+a)=a^2-1$ gives $|z|\le1$.
The choice $z=0$ gives $x=y=0$, whereas $z=1$ gives
\[
x-b+a(y+a)=0.
\]
Here $1\le y+a\le2a-1$ and $b-x\le b+a-1\le2a-3$, so necessarily
$y+a=1$ and $x=b-a=-C$; the case $z=-1$ is the negative solution.
For $k=1$, if $z\le a-2$, then
\[
x+ay+Q_2z
\le d(1+a)+(a-2)Q_2
=Q_3-\bigl(a(a-b)-2b+1\bigr)<Q_3,
\]
because $a-b\ge2$.  Thus $z=d$, and
\[
x+ay=a(a-b)-b=aC-b.
\]
Reduction modulo $a$, together with $|x|\le a-1$, leaves precisely
$(x,y)=(-b,C)$ and $(x,y)=(C,C-1)$.  Hence the complete list is
\[
(x,y,z;k)\in
\{(0,0,0;0),\ \pm(C,d,-1;0),\
  \ \pm(-b,C,d;1),\ \pm(C,C-1,d;1)\}.
\]
Its terminal pairs are $(0,0)$, $\pm(d,-1)$, $\pm(C,d)$, and
$\pm(C-1,d)$.  Since $2\le C\le d$, neither $\pm(-b,C)$ nor
$\pm(C,C-1)$ occurs in this list of terminal pairs.  Thus a $t=2$ triple
of either of the last two types cannot overlap a $t=1$ triple.  The only
remaining nonzero possibility is $\pm(C,d,-1)$; it forces the $t=1$
triple to be $\pm(-b,C,d)$, whose initial pair $\pm(-b,C)$ cannot overlap
a $t=0$ triple.  Therefore $e_4=0$ also in this chamber.

In either sign, every nonzero triple in the corresponding list has nonzero
last coordinate.  It follows successively from the equations at
$t=2,1,0$ that $e_2=e_3=e_4=0$, then $e_1=0$, and finally $e_0=0$.
Thus the kernel is trivial also at $m=3$.  The finite computation described in
the data-availability statement includes this case as regression testing; it
is not used as proof of the parameter-uniform statement.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Sharp threshold and extremal bases}

\begin{theorem}[Full quadratic-Pisot cyclic-rank threshold]
\label{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold}
Let $\beta$ be an arbitrary quadratic Pisot number and use exactly the
greedy language, Parry data, rank, cyclic modulus and fold of
Definition~\ref{def:quadratic-parry-fold}.  Then
\[
m_*(\beta)=
\begin{cases}
3,&\beta\text{ is the root of }x^2-ax-a,\quad a\ge1,\\
3,&\beta\text{ is the root of }x^2-ax+1,\quad a\ge3,\\
2,&\text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]
More strongly, for every $m\ge m_*(\beta)$ and every $n\ge m$, the
finite-block sliding fold is injective and
\begin{equation}
|\mathcal L_n(Y_{\beta,m})|
=(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)^{n+m-1}.
\label{eq:quadratic-pisot-block-count}
\end{equation}
At these apertures $\Phi_{\beta,m}$ is a topological conjugacy from the full

$$d_\beta(1)=t_1t_2\cdots t_p0^\infty,\qquad

ä¸æ˜¯  $[-2d,2d]$ ã€‚
ç›¸åº”è¯­è¨€è®¡æ•°æ»¡è¶³

$$Q_0=1,$$


$$Q_n=1+\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_iQ_{n-i}\quad(1\le n<p),
\qquad
Q_n=\sum_{i=1}^{p}t_iQ_{n-i}\quad(n\ge p).$$

è¿™ä¸€ Parryâ€“Bertrand æƒåºåˆ—åŠå…¶åˆæ³•è¡¨ç¤ºè¯­è¨€å‚è§ Charlierâ€“Cisterninoâ€“Stipulantiã€‚ä»¥ä¸‹å‡å®šæœ¬æ–‡æ‰€ç”¨ colex ç§©æ»¡è¶³

ä¸”åœ¨åˆæ³•é•¿  $m$  è¯ä¸Šä¾æ¬¡å–å€¼  $0,\ldots,Q_m-1$ ã€‚

å¯¹  $F(z)=\sum f_jz^j$ ï¼Œå®šä¹‰

$$\partial F=\frac{F-F(0)}z,\qquad

$$\Lambda_m(F)

å¯¹  $L\ge1$ ï¼Œå®šä¹‰åæ»‘åŠ¨åŒä½™é›†

$$\mathcal N_{\beta,m,L}

ç­‰ä»·åœ°ï¼Œä»¤  $T_{m,L}$  ä¸ºå¸¦çŠ¶ Toeplitz çŸ©é˜µ

$$T_{m,L}=

å®šç†ï¼šç²¾ç¡®å› æžœåˆ»ç”»

å…¶ä¸­è‹¥æ‰€æœ‰  $\mathcal N_{\beta,m,L}$  å‡éžç©ºï¼Œåˆ™å³ç«¯å®šä¹‰ä¸º  $\infty$ ã€‚
è¯æ˜Ž
è®¾ä¸¤ä¸ªåŽŸå§‹å—  $u,v\in A_d^{m+L-1}$  äº§ç”Ÿç›¸åŒçš„  $L$  ä¸ªè¿žç»­è¾“å‡ºï¼Œä»¤  $e_i=u_i-v_i$ ã€‚colex ç§©åœ¨åˆæ³•ä»£è¡¨ä¸Šä¸ºåŒå°„ï¼Œæ•…ç¬¬  $t$  ä¸ªè¾“å‡ºç›¸ç­‰å½“ä¸”ä»…å½“

证明
定义整数线性泛函
L:Z[z]⟶Z,L(j∑​rj​zj)=j∑​rj​Qj​.

有界边界余式消除

则
P∣R.(4)
事实上，将 R 除以首一多项式 P，可唯一写成
R=PH+u+vz,H∈Z[z].
由 (2) 以及 Q0​=1,Q1​=a+1，
0=L(R)=u+(a+1)v.
故存在 τ∈Z，使
R=PH+τ(z−(a+1)).(5)
设 τ=0。同时改变 R,H,τ 的符号后，可设 τ>0。写
H(z)=j≥0∑​hj​zj,
并约定充分大的 j 满足 hj​=0。定义交错商进位
uj​=(−1)j+1hj​.(6)

从而 u0​≥1；继而
bu1​=au0​+τ−r1​≥a+1−a=1,
从而 u1​≥1。
对每个 j≥2，比较 zj 的系数给出
rj​=(−1)j−1(uj−2​+auj−1​−buj​).(8)
若 uj−2​,uj−1​≥1，则由 ∣rj​∣≤a，
buj​≥uj−2​+auj−1​−a≥1.(9)
因此归纳得到
uj​≥1(j≥0).(10)
然而 H 为多项式，故充分大的 j 应有 uj​=0，矛盾。于是 τ=0，从而 P∣R。
这里的 (6)–(10) 即所需的参数一致有界进位不变量：任何非零常数边界进位都会强制交错商进位始终为正，因而不可能在有限位置终止。它不依赖枚举，并覆盖商多项式的全部整数分支。

故

于是

# Literature and novelty check: quadratic-Pisot Parry-rank folds

Checked: 2026-08-02 (Asia/Singapore).

## arXiv API query log

The required first-party Atom API was queried directly at
`https://export.arxiv.org/api/query` with the following searches:

- `all:(beta-expansion OR beta-numeration OR Pisot substitution)`, `max_results=50`;
- `au:Frougny`, `max_results=5`;
- `all:beta-expansion`, `max_results=10`.

Every request reached arXiv but returned HTTP `429` with
`cache-control: private, no-store` (Varnish edge: Singapore, upstream LGA).
Three delayed retries of the broad query returned the same status.  This is
recorded as an API availability limitation, not treated as a zero-result
novelty search.  To avoid a false novelty assertion, the audit was completed
against arXiv's first-party HTML search and abstract metadata, with DOI
metadata cross-checked through Crossref.

First-party arXiv exact-topic searches returned zero records for each of:

- `"finite-window beta fold"`;
- `"sliding-window beta normalization conjugacy"`;
- `"quadratic Pisot window threshold"`;
- `"Parry fold conjugacy"`;
- `"beta expansion sliding block conjugacy"`.

This supports only the narrow statement that no arXiv record located by these
queries states the paper's cyclic Parry-rank sliding-fold threshold.  It is
not proof that no unpublished or differently worded result exists.

## Closest beta-expansion and normalization literature

1. W. Parry, *On the beta-expansions of real numbers*, Acta Math. Acad. Sci.
   Hungar. **11** (1960), 401-416. DOI:
   `10.1007/BF02020954`. No arXiv record (publication predates arXiv).
   Used for the lexicographic admissibility criterion and `d_beta^*(1)`;
   the paper does not study cyclic finite-window folds or overlap thresholds.

2. C. Frougny, *Representations of numbers and finite automata*, Math.
   Systems Theory **25** (1992), 37-60. DOI:
   `10.1007/BF01305290`. No arXiv record located. Used only for the established
   finite-automaton normalization context; its automata are not presented as
   the cyclic rank-modulo-`Q_m` maps classified here.

3. C. Frougny and W. Steiner, *Minimal weight expansions in Pisot bases*,
   J. Math. Cryptology **2** (2008), 365-392. arXiv:`0803.2874`;
   DOI:`10.1515/JMC.2008.017`. It proves automaton recognizability for
   minimal-weight expansions in Pisot bases, not the present greedy
   Parry-rank fold or a sharp window threshold.

4. V. Berthe, C. Frougny, M. Rigo and J. Sakarovitch, *The carry propagation
   of the successor function*, Adv. Appl. Math. **120** (2020), 102062.
   arXiv:`1907.01464`; DOI:`10.1016/j.aam.2020.102062`. It studies amortized
   carry propagation in several numeration systems, including beta-numeration;
   it does not classify sliding-window conjugacy.

5. T. Hejda and W. Steiner, *Beta-expansions of rational numbers in quadratic
   Pisot bases*, Acta Arith. **183** (2018), 35-51.
   arXiv:`1411.2419`; DOI:`10.4064/aa8260-11-2017`. This is the closest
   full-quadratic-Pisot parameter comparison found. It studies purely periodic
   rational beta-expansions and computes `gamma(beta)`, not finite-window
   normalization or overlap injectivity.

6. B. Adamczewski, C. Frougny, A. Siegel and W. Steiner, *Rational numbers
   with purely periodic beta-expansion*, Bull. Lond. Math. Soc. **42** (2010),
   538-552. arXiv:`0907.0206`; DOI:`10.1112/blms/bdq019`. Relevant to
   periodic beta-expansions and Pisot-unit restrictions, but not to the cyclic
   fold threshold.

7. C. Kalle and W. Steiner, *Beta-expansions, natural extensions and multiple
   tilings associated with Pisot units*, Trans. Amer. Math. Soc. **364**
   (2012), 2281-2318. arXiv:`0907.2676`;
   DOI:`10.1090/S0002-9947-2012-05362-1`. It develops natural extensions and
   tilings arising from greedy beta-transformations; the abstract explicitly
   traces that geometric construction to Rauzy and Thurston. It does not
   contain the rank-modulo finite-window code studied here.

8. M. Minervino and W. Steiner, *Tilings for Pisot beta numeration*,
   arXiv:`1310.1277`. This treats Rauzy/beta tiles, weak finiteness, and purely
   periodic expansions for non-unit Pisot numbers; no DOI was exposed in the
   arXiv metadata checked. It supplies context for non-unit bases, not the
   claimed threshold.

## Thurston and Pisot-substitution boundary

W. Thurston's *Groups, tilings, and finite state automata* (AMS Colloquium
Lectures, 1989) is an unpublished lecture manuscript commonly cited for
beta-tiles; no arXiv ID or DOI was located.  The present manuscript therefore
does not assign it a fabricated identifier.  The traceable modern source used
for the Rauzy-Thurston statement is Kalle-Steiner, arXiv:`0907.2676`, DOI
`10.1090/S0002-9947-2012-05362-1`.

For the broader substitution interface, representative records are:

- M. Minervino and J. Thuswaldner, *The geometry of non-unit Pisot
  substitutions*, arXiv:`1402.2002`, Ann. Inst. Fourier **64** (2014),
  1373-1417, DOI:`10.5802/aif.2884`;
- V. Berthe, W. Steiner and J. Thuswaldner, *Geometry, dynamics, and
  arithmetic of S-adic shifts*, arXiv:`1410.0331`, Ann. Inst. Fourier **69**
  (2019), 1347-1409, DOI:`10.5802/aif.3273`;
- K. Nakaishi, *Pisot Substitution Conjecture and Rauzy Fractals*,
  arXiv:`2401.07771` (no DOI in the checked arXiv metadata).

These concern Rauzy fractals, substitutions, tilings, or spectral questions.
They are not predecessors of the cyclic finite-window rank fold.  Accordingly
the theorem is explicitly restricted to greedy quadratic beta-shifts and does
not claim a threshold for general Pisot substitutions.

## Novelty conclusion and limitation

The exact result not found in the checked records is the classification of the
least nontrivial aperture at which the cyclic Parry-rank fold from the full
digit shift is a conjugacy onto its SFT image, together with the two extremal
quadratic-Pisot loci.  Parry admissibility, Pisot normalization automata,
periodic expansions, beta-tiles, and Pisot-substitution geometry are cited as
prior results and are not reproved as new.  Because the Atom API itself was
rate-limited, the novelty conclusion remains a documented search conclusion,
not a claim of exhaustive bibliographic nonexistence.

Checked: 2026-08-03 (Asia/Singapore).  The first-party Atom endpoint was
available for this audit.  Each request returned HTTP 200.  The exact query
`all:"cyclic rank recoding"` returned zero records.  The following broader

- `all:("Fischer cover" AND "full shift")`;
- `all:("Parry" AND "quadratic Pisot" AND language)`;
- `all:("beta-shifts" AND "same language")`.

\begin{definition}[Finite-alphabet readout]\label{def:readout}
Let $(H,\mathcal B,\mu,T)$ be a probability-preserving dynamical system and let
$\mathcal A$ be a finite alphabet.  A finite-alphabet readout is a measurable
map $R:H\to\mathcal A$.  It produces the one-sided symbolic process
\[
  \mathcal C_R(h):=(R(T^t h))_{t\ge0}\in\mathcal A^{\NN}
\]
and, when $T$ is invertible, the two-sided process
\[
  \mathcal C_R(h):=(R(T^t h))_{t\in\ZZ}\in\mathcal A^{\ZZ}.
\]
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Coding map and induced input law]\label{def:coding-law}
For a readout $R$ as in Definition~\ref{def:readout}, the coding map is
$\mathcal C_R$.  The induced symbolic input law is the pushforward
\[
  \nu_R:=(\mathcal C_R)_*\mu
\]
on the corresponding full shift over $\mathcal A$.  Its length-$m$ marginal is
denoted by $\nu_{R,m}$.
\end{definition}
\begin{proposition}[Stationarity and ergodicity of the induced law]\label{prop:induced-law}
If $T$ preserves $\mu$, then $\nu_R$ is shift-invariant.  If, in addition,
$(H,\mathcal B,\mu,T)$ is ergodic, then $\nu_R$ is ergodic.
This standard source-law fact is used only to set the appendix model and is
not an input to the threshold, metallic, or Fischer-cover theorems.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Rotation-to-grammar bridge in the golden regime]\label{prop:sturmian-no11}
Let $\alpha=(\sqrt5-1)/2$ and let $T_\alpha x=x+\alpha\pmod 1$ on $\TT$.
With the binary readout
\[
R(x)=\ind_{[0,\,1-\alpha)}(x),
\]
the induced language is Sturmian and every word in it belongs to the
golden-mean language: no factor contains the block $11$.
This is a source-model bridge only; the finite-window normalization is defined
on the full binary cube independently of this Sturmian restriction.
\end{proposition}
\begin{remark}[Sturmian input]
Proposition~\ref{prop:sturmian-no11} shows that a noiseless Fibonacci source lies in the admissible language~$X_m$. Together with Theorem~\ref{thm:fold-suite}(ii), this means that local stabilization acts as the identity on the noiseless source. The model remains useful because it supplies the grammar of the normalization, and its linear word complexity gives the total-variation bound in Section~\ref{sec:fp:tv}.
\end{remark}
\begin{theorem}[Positive local noise gives full support]\label{thm:noise-full-support}
Let $\lambda$ be any law on $\mathcal A^{\ZZ}$ and let a binary noisy readout
be obtained by passing each input symbol independently through a channel
$K:\mathcal A\times\{0,1\}\to(0,1)$.  If
$K(a,b)>0$ for every $a\in\mathcal A$ and $b\in\{0,1\}$, then every binary
word of every finite length has positive probability under the output law.
This is the full-support condition motivating use of the full cube; it is a
standard readout assumption, not a new structural result about $\Fold_m$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proposition}[Finite-radius detector realization of full support]\label{prop:detector-realization}
The same full-support conclusion holds for any finite-radius binary detector
whose local draws are conditionally independent given the input contexts and
whose conditional output probabilities are uniformly positive.  More
precisely, suppose the output symbol at time $t$ is generated from an input
context $C_t$ of radius $\rho$, the output draws are conditionally independent
given $(C_t)_{t\in\ZZ}$, and
\[
  \inf_{c}\min\{P(0\mid c),P(1\mid c)\}>0 .
\]
Then every binary word of every finite length has positive output probability.
\end{proposition}
\begin{remark}
Theorem~\ref{thm:noise-full-support} gives the memoryless statement, while Proposition~\ref{prop:detector-realization} gives the finite-radius analogue. Thus, after strictly positive local uncertainty is introduced at the readout layer, length-$m$ words need not remain in the Sturmian language.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[Fiber-uniform lift]\label{def:fiber-lift}
Let $\mu$ be a probability measure on $\{0,1\}^m$ and put
$\pi=(\Fold_m)_*\mu$.  The fiber-uniform lift of $\pi$ is the probability
measure $\mu^e$ on $\{0,1\}^m$ defined by
\[
  \mu^e(\omega)
  :=
  \frac{\pi(\Fold_m(\omega))}{d_m(\Fold_m(\omega))},
  \qquad \omega\in\{0,1\}^m .
\]
Equivalently, $\mu^e$ has visible law $\pi$ and is uniform on each fiber
$\mathcal F_m(x)$.
\end{definition}
\begin{remark}
The normalization is immediate:
\[
\sum_{\omega\in\{0,1\}^m}\mu^e(\omega)
=
\sum_{x\in X_m}\sum_{\omega\in\mathcal{F}_m(x)}\frac{\pi(x)}{|\mathcal{F}_m(x)|}
=
\sum_{x\in X_m}\pi(x)=1.
\]
\end{remark}
\begin{theorem}[Entropy decomposition over fibers]\label{thm:kl-ledger}
Let $\mu$ be a probability measure on $\{0,1\}^m$, let
$\pi=(\Fold_m)_*\mu$, and let $\mu^e$ be the fiber-uniform lift of $\pi$.
Then
\[
H(\mu)
=
H(\pi)+\EE_\pi\!\left[
H\bigl(\mu(\,\cdot\,\mid\mathcal F_m(X))\bigr)
\right],
\]
\[
H(\mu^e)=H(\pi)+\EE_\pi[\log d_m(X)],
\]
and
\[
D_{\KL}(\mu\|\mu^e)
=
\EE_\pi[\log d_m(X)]
-
\EE_\pi\!\left[
H\bigl(\mu(\,\cdot\,\mid\mathcal F_m(X))\bigr)
\right].
\]
This is a fixed-window finite-partition identity and is not used to prove
path-space injectivity or the Fischer-cover lower bound.
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Entropy increase under fiber equilibration]\label{cor:entropy-increase}
Let $\mu^e$ be the fiber-uniform lift of $(\Fold_m)_*\mu$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:entropy-increase}
H(\mu^e)-H(\mu)=D_{\KL}(\mu\|\mu^e).
\end{equation}
This corollary records the same fixed-window bookkeeping and makes no
trajectory-level information-loss claim.
\end{corollary}
\begin{remark}
Theorem~\ref{thm:kl-ledger} separates three contributions: the visible
entropy $H(\pi)$ of the stabilized type, the logarithmic fiber size
$\EE_\pi[\log d_m(X)]$, and the within-fiber divergence
$D_{\KL}(\mu\|\mu^e)$. For uniform input on $\{0,1\}^m$, one has
$\pi_m^{\mathrm{unif}}(x)=d_m(x)/2^m$; hence the fiber counts determine both
the visible law and the collision probability in Section~\ref{sec:fiber-spectrum}.
\end{remark}
\begin{remark}
Theorem~\ref{thm:kl-ledger} is a fixed-window identity. For $m\ge3$, Theorem~\ref{thm:two-sided-conjugacy} shows that this local multiplicity does not imply loss of information on path space, because the overlapping process has a finite-memory inverse.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Equivalence of block presentations above threshold]\label{thm:exact-sufficiency}
Assume $m\ge3$ and $n\ge m$.  Let
\[
E_{m,n}:=\{0,1\}^{n+m-1}
\]
be the raw block experiment and let $\mathcal Y_{m,n}$ be the stabilized
block experiment.  Then the deterministic channel
\[
\Phi_{m,n}:E_{m,n}\to\mathcal Y_{m,n}
\]
is a Blackwell equivalence.  More explicitly, the forward kernel is
$K(y\mid a)=\ind_{\{\Phi_{m,n}(a)=y\}}$ and the reverse kernel is
$L(a\mid y)=\ind_{\{\Psi_{m,n}(y)=a\}}$, and
\[
LK=\operatorname{id}_{E_{m,n}},\qquad KL=\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal Y_{m,n}}.
\]
Consequently every finite decision problem has the same value from the raw and
stabilized block observations.  In particular, for probability laws $\mu,\nu$
on $E_{m,n}$ and their pushforwards
$\eta=(\Phi_{m,n})_*\mu$, $\xi=(\Phi_{m,n})_*\nu$,
\[
D_{\TV}(\eta,\xi)=D_{\TV}(\mu,\nu)
\]
and
\[
D_{\KL}(\eta\|\xi)=D_{\KL}(\mu\|\nu),
\]
with the convention that both sides are $+\infty$ when absolute continuity
fails.
This is a standard finite-block consequence of the already proved bijection
$\Phi_{m,n}:E_{m,n}\to\mathcal Y_{m,n}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Path-space equivalence and relative entropy rates]\label{cor:path-space-divergence}
Assume $m\ge3$.  Let $\mu$ and $\nu$ be shift-invariant Borel probability
measures on $\{0,1\}^{\ZZ}$, and set
\[
\eta:=(\Phi_m)_*\mu,\qquad \xi:=(\Phi_m)_*\nu .
\]
Then $\Phi_m$ is a bimeasurable isomorphism from
$(\{0,1\}^{\ZZ},\mu,\sigma)$ to $(Y_m,\eta,\sigma)$, with inverse given by the
local decoder in Theorem~\ref{thm:two-sided-conjugacy}.  Moreover, for every
$N\ge m$,
\[
D_{\KL}\bigl(\eta_{[0,N-1]}\|\xi_{[0,N-1]}\bigr)
=
D_{\KL}\bigl(\mu_{[0,N+m-2]}\|\nu_{[0,N+m-2]}\bigr),
\]
with both sides allowed to be $+\infty$.  Without an additional hypothesis,
define the upper and lower relative-entropy rates by limsup and liminf.  They
are transported separately:
\begin{align*}
\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac1N
D_{\KL}\bigl(\eta_{[0,N-1]}\|\xi_{[0,N-1]}\bigr)
&=
\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac1N
D_{\KL}\bigl(\mu_{[0,N-1]}\|\nu_{[0,N-1]}\bigr),\\
\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac1N
D_{\KL}\bigl(\eta_{[0,N-1]}\|\xi_{[0,N-1]}\bigr)
&=
\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac1N
D_{\KL}\bigl(\mu_{[0,N-1]}\|\nu_{[0,N-1]}\bigr).
\end{align*}
In particular, a limit on one side exists if and only if it exists on the
other, and then the limits agree.  One sufficient condition is that the
reference law $\nu$ be a stationary finite-order Markov law with stationary
transition probabilities; under that additional hypothesis the extended
limit exists \cite[Chapter~8]{Gray2011}.
\end{corollary}
\begin{remark}[Local fibers versus trajectory information]
The entropy identity in Theorem~\ref{thm:kl-ledger} concerns a single
window and records unresolved multiplicity inside the finite fibers of
$\Fold_m$. Corollary~\ref{cor:path-space-divergence} is a different statement:
overlapping windows carry enough consistency information to reconstruct the
whole trajectory above the threshold.
\end{remark}
\begin{theorem}[Transport of continuous potentials under the conjugacy]\label{thm:continuous-potentials}
Assume $m\ge3$ and let $f:\{0,1\}^{\ZZ}\to\RR$ be continuous.  Define the
transported potential on $Y_m$ by
\[
\widetilde f(y):=f(\Phi_m^{-1}y).
\]
Then
\[
P_{Y_m}(\widetilde f)=P_{\{0,1\}^{\ZZ}}(f),
\]
where pressure is taken with respect to the shift.  Under the pushforward
correspondence $\mu\mapsto(\Phi_m)_*\mu$, the equilibrium states for $f$ on
the full two-shift are exactly the equilibrium states for $\widetilde f$ on
$Y_m$.
This is the usual pressure invariance under topological conjugacy; no new
Fibonacci arithmetic is used beyond the decoder establishing the conjugacy.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proposition}[Finite-range energetics under local inversion]\label{prop:finite-range-renorm}
Assume $m\ge3$ and let $\phi:\{0,1\}^r\to\RR$ be a finite-range interaction
of range $r\ge1$.  Define
\[
\widetilde\phi:\mathcal L_{r+m-1}(Y_m)\to\RR
\]
by
\[
\widetilde\phi(y_0,\ldots,y_{r+m-2})
:=
\phi\bigl(
\psi_m(y_0,\ldots,y_{m-1}),
\psi_m(y_1,\ldots,y_m),
\ldots,
\psi_m(y_{r-1},\ldots,y_{r+m-2})
\bigr).
\]
Then $\widetilde\phi$ has stabilized range at most $r+m-1$, and for every
$a\in\{0,1\}^{\ZZ}$, $y=\Phi_m(a)$, and $t\in\ZZ$,
\[
\phi(a_t,\ldots,a_{t+r-1})
=
\widetilde\phi(y_t,\ldots,y_{t+r+m-2}).
\]
Consequently every raw finite-range energy sum is represented exactly as a
finite-range energy sum on the stabilized trajectory:
\[
\sum_{t=p}^{q}\phi(a_t,\ldots,a_{t+r-1})
=
\sum_{t=p}^{q}\widetilde\phi(y_t,\ldots,y_{t+r+m-2})
\]
for all integers $p\le q$.
This is a bookkeeping consequence of the local inverse, included only to
record how finite-range observables are transported.
\end{proposition}
\begin{remark}[Prototype observables]
Taking $\phi(a_0):=a_0$ gives exact recovery of occupancy sums, and taking
$\phi(a_0,a_1):=a_0a_1$ gives exact recovery of nearest-neighbour pair sums.
Thus the occupancy and nearest-neighbour energy examples used below are
instances of Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-range-renorm}; they do not require
additional thermodynamic assumptions.
\end{remark}

 succeeded in 204ms:
\begin{proposition}[Second-moment linear recurrence]\label{prop:second-moment-recurrence}
For all $m\ge4$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sigma-recurrence}
\Sigma_m=2\Sigma_{m-1}+2\Sigma_{m-2}-2\Sigma_{m-3},
\end{equation}
with initial values $\Sigma_1=2$, $\Sigma_2=6$, and $\Sigma_3=14$.
This is the independent finite-window statistic retained from the auxiliary
material; it is not used in the proof of the sharp overlap threshold.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Uniform collision recurrence]\label{prop:uniform-collision}
Under the uniform raw law on $\{0,1\}^m$, the collision probability
\[
\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(m):=
\sum_{x\in X_m}\pi_m^{\mathrm{unif}}(x)^2
\]
of $\pi_m^{\mathrm{unif}}:=(\Fold_m)_*\mu_m^{\mathrm{unif}}$ satisfies
$\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)=\Sigma_m/2^{2m}$ and therefore, for $m\ge4$,
\[
\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)
=\frac12\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(m-1)
+\frac18\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(m-2)
-\frac1{32}\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(m-3),
\]
with initial values
\[
\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(1)=\frac12,\qquad
\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(2)=\frac38,\qquad
\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(3)=\frac7{32}.
\]
This is only the collision-probability restatement of the preceding
second-moment recurrence.
\end{proposition}
\begin{remark}
The fiber-count identity is immediate from Definition~\ref{def:fold} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:fiber-residue}: under the uniform raw law,
$\pi_m^{\mathrm{unif}}(x)=|\mathcal F_m(x)|/2^m=d_m(x)/2^m$, and hence
$\Col_{\mathrm{unif}}(m)=2^{-2m}\sum_{x\in X_m}d_m(x)^2$. The increment here
is the closed scalar recurrence for the uniform collision probability,
obtained by combining that identity with
Proposition~\ref{prop:second-moment-recurrence}.
\end{remark}

 succeeded in 200ms:
\begin{proposition}[Dynamical corollaries above threshold]
\label{prop:conjugacy-consequences}
For $m\ge3$, the conjugacy $\Phi_m$ identifies invariant Borel probability
measures on the full two-shift and on $Y_m$, preserving ergodicity, mixing
properties, Bernoulli-ness, the $K$-property, and entropy.  Moreover,
\[
\#\operatorname{Fix}(\sigma^n|_{Y_m})=2^n,
\qquad
\zeta_{Y_m}(z)=\frac1{1-2z}.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Entropy preservation for every aperture]
\label{prop:measure-entropy-preservation}
Let $m\ge2$, let $\nu$ be shift invariant on $\{0,1\}^{\mathbb Z}$, and put
$\eta=(\Phi_m)_*\nu$.  Then $h_\eta(\sigma)=h_\nu(\sigma)$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{corollary}[Topological entropy]
\label{cor:entropy-preservation}
For every $m\ge2$, $h_{\rm top}(Y_m)=\log2$.
\end{corollary}

 succeeded in 211ms:
\begin{theorem}[Stabilized block law from discrepancy]\label{thm:tv-certificate}
Let $D_N^*(\alpha;x_0)$ denote the interval discrepancy of the orbit segment
$\{x_0+t\alpha\}_{t=0}^{N-1}$, namely the supremum over circle intervals $I$
of
\[
\left|
\frac1N\#\{0\le t<N:\{x_0+t\alpha\}\in I\}-|I|
\right|.
\]
Then, for every $m\ge2$ and $n\ge1$,
\[
D_{\TV}\bigl(\hat{\eta}_{m,n,N},\eta_{m,n}\bigr)
\le (m+n)D_N^*(\alpha;x_0).
\]
This is a supporting rotation-source certificate; the structural conjugacy
results above do not depend on it.
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Bound for bounded stabilized-block observables]\label{cor:observable-certificate}
If $g:\mathcal Y_{m,n}\to\RR$ is bounded, then
\[
\left|
\int g\,d\hat{\eta}_{m,n,N}-\int g\,d\eta_{m,n}
\right|
\le
2\|g\|_\infty (m+n)D_N^*(\alpha;x_0).
\]
This is only the bounded-observable form of the preceding supporting
rotation-source certificate.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proposition}[Boundary-layer control for raw and stabilized block laws]\label{prop:boundary-layer}
Assume that $T$ is measure preserving and that
$\mu_L^{(0)}$ and $\mu_L^{(\rho)}$ are the laws of the sharp and blurred
length-$L$ words obtained from the same random initial point $h\sim\mu$ along
the common orbit
\[
h,Th,\ldots,T^{L-1}h .
\]
With $L=m+n-1$,
\[
D_{\TV}\bigl(\mu_L^{(\rho)},\mu_L^{(0)}\bigr)
\le L\varepsilon_\rho
\]
and therefore
\[
D_{\TV}\bigl(\eta_{m,n}^{(\rho)},\eta_{m,n}^{(0)}\bigr)
\le (m+n-1)\varepsilon_\rho .
\]
The statement is a conditional boundary-layer estimate for readout models and
does not assert that all blurred readouts have interval atom partitions.
\end{proposition}
\begin{corollary}[Conditional interval-partition resolution certificate]\label{cor:resolution-certificate}
The following is a conditional certificate. The blurred readout need not be a
two-interval coding, but assume that its depth-$(m+n-1)$ atom partition consists
of at most $m+n$ intervals, so that its empirical raw-word law differs from its
limiting raw-word law in total variation by at most
$(m+n)D_N^*(\alpha;x_0)$. Then, for the empirical blurred stabilized law
$\hat{\eta}_{m,n,N}^{(\rho)}$,
\[
D_{\TV}\bigl(\hat{\eta}_{m,n,N}^{(\rho)},\eta_{m,n}^{(0)}\bigr)
\le
(m+n)D_N^*(\alpha;x_0)+(m+n-1)\varepsilon_\rho.
\]
\end{corollary}
\begin{remark}
For the sharp two-interval Sturmian coding, the depth-$(m+n-1)$ atoms form at
most $m+n$ intervals, so the same argument gives the displayed certificate
with $\varepsilon_\rho=0$.  The present statement records the
interval-partition extension: the certificate holds for any blurred readout
whose depth atoms form at most $m+n$ intervals, even when the blurred readout
is not itself a two-interval coding.
\end{remark}
\begin{theorem}[Recovery of finite-range observables from stabilized blocks]\label{thm:operational-observable-recovery}
Assume $m\ge3$ and $n\ge m$.  Let
$f:\{0,1\}^{m+n-1}\to\RR$ be any finite-range raw observable.  There is a
unique observable $\widetilde f:\mathcal Y_{m,n}\to\RR$ such that
\[
f=\widetilde f\circ\Phi_{m,n}
\quad\text{on }\{0,1\}^{m+n-1}.
\]
It is given by $\widetilde f(y)=f(\Psi_{m,n}(y))$, where
$\Psi_{m,n}$ is the inverse block decoder from
Theorem~\ref{thm:block-invertibility}.  Consequently
\[
\int f\,d\hat{\mu}_{m+n-1,N}
=
\int \widetilde f\,d\hat{\eta}_{m,n,N},
\qquad
\int f\,d\mu_{m+n-1}
=
\int \widetilde f\,d\eta_{m,n},
\]
and
\[
\left|
\int f\,d\hat{\mu}_{m+n-1,N}
-
\int f\,d\mu_{m+n-1}
\right|
\le
2\|f\|_\infty (m+n)D_N^*(\alpha;x_0).
\]
This is an operational restatement of the finite-block inverse together with
the supporting discrepancy bound.
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Bounded-type slopes]\label{cor:tv-bounded-type}
If $\alpha$ has bounded continued-fraction partial quotients, then there is a
constant $C_\alpha$ such that, for all $N\ge2$,
\[
D_{\TV}\bigl(\hat{\eta}_{m,n,N},\eta_{m,n}\bigr)
\le
C_\alpha(m+n)\frac{\log N}{N}.
\]
For $m\ge3$ and $n\ge m$, every recovered finite-range observable in
Theorem~\ref{thm:operational-observable-recovery} satisfies the corresponding
bound
\[
\left|
\int f\,d\hat{\mu}_{m+n-1,N}
-
\int f\,d\mu_{m+n-1}
\right|
\le
2C_\alpha\|f\|_\infty(m+n)\frac{\log N}{N}.
\]
This corollary only substitutes the classical bounded-type rotation
discrepancy estimate into the preceding support results.
\end{corollary}
\begin{remark}
Theorem~\ref{thm:operational-observable-recovery} and Corollary~\ref{cor:resolution-certificate} quantify the rotation source law after the conjugacy has been established. The finite-sample error terms are the orbit discrepancy and the boundary-layer mass.
\end{remark}

\section*{Data and code availability}

No external data sets were used.  The proofs in the paper are analytic; the
finite computations supplied with the source are regression tests and are not
certificates of any parameter-uniform theorem.

The accompanying source archive contains
\path{artifacts/verify_metallic_threshold.py},
\path{artifacts/verify_quadratic_pisot_threshold.py},
\path{artifacts/test_quadratic_pisot_threshold.py},
\path{artifacts/verify_simple_parry_causal.py},
\path{artifacts/test_verify_simple_parry_causal.py}, and saved text output.
The quadratic rank battery tests lengths
$1\le m\le5$ for the negative chamber
$1\le a\le6$, $1\le b\le a$, and for the positive chamber
$3\le a\le8$, $1\le b\le a-2$.  The block-language battery tests
$m=n\in\{2,3,4\}$ on the same 42 parameter pairs.  Additional tests use
$n=m+1$ for six boundary/interior representatives through $m=4$, and periodic
counts through period six for four extremal representatives.
The extended local-structure battery checks all 15 dual chamber pairs with
$3\le q\le7$ at apertures two and three, the exact causal lengths and
finite-block onset through $m=5$ for four representatives, and the critical
periodic-fiber histogram through period six for four extremal representatives.
The simple-Parry battery checks the Parry recurrences and consecutive colex
ranks on five finite Parry words through length six, compares Toeplitz bad
blocks with collision-graph path counts in three systems, exhausts the
$p$-bonacci causal calculation for $3\le p\le8$, and enumerates all bad
blocks through four outputs for the cubic word $101$ at aperture four.  It
also checks the explicit period-one and period-four collision witnesses at
apertures two and three for that cubic base.  The extended graph battery
checks the unique zero predecessor, injectivity--causality equivalence,
state-count bound, and bounded periodic witnesses in three representative
systems.  It also checks all three aperture-two regimes and the quadratic
specializations on five finite Parry words.
The metallic
script tests the main cases $1\le A\le4$, $2\le m\le5$, together with
$A\in\{5,6\}$, $2\le m\le4$; its extended block tests use $A=1$,
$3\le m\le5$, together with $2\le A\le4$, $2\le m\le4$.  It also checks
the golden block-bijection identity for $3\le m\le6$ at $n=m,m+1$ and
periodic counts through period six for $1\le A\le4$ at $m=2$.

These internal paths identify files in the review bundle only; they are not
presented as persistent URLs.  A versioned public archival release and its DOI
have not yet been assigned and must be supplied before final publication.

+请以当前事实边界为准，判断本稿已建立的工具在相邻文献中真正能够触及的最强定理，而非复核或增补现稿。
+核心问题是：是否存在一个有明确名称、提出者及原始出处的公开问题，可由下述工具形成非循环、可证伪的攻击路线？
+本文研究的不是数值 β-正规化，而是如下 sliding block code：对 greedy β-shift 的长度 m 语言作 colexicographic 整数排名，
+将同一权重和延拓到完整数字立方体，模合法词数 Q_m 取余，再以唯一合法词代表该余类，并沿输入序列重叠滑动。
+已证结果一：对任意二次 Pisot 数，首次成为到其 SFT 像的拓扑共轭的非平凡窗口长度仅为 2 或 3；
+其值为 3 当且仅当最小多项式为 x^2-ax-a（a≥1）或 x^2-ax+1（a≥3），其余情形为 2。
+阈值以上，长度 n≥m 的有限块映射单射，像语言复杂度为 (⌊β⌋+1)^(n+m-1)，逆码具有零记忆和有限预见。
+并已证有限块单射首次出现于 n=m，像在固定输出字母表中恰为 m-step 而非 (m-1)-step SFT，自然重叠图为右 Fischer cover。
+已证结果二：对任意非整数 simple-Parry 数（无需 Pisot 性），未来单侧解码延迟恰等于有限差分碰撞图中坏初态的最长存活路径。
+在此类中，双边单射等价于有限未来解码；不单射时存在周期不超过 (2d+1)^(m-1)-1 的碰撞证书，窗口 m=2 已完全三分。
+已证结果三：存在显式三次 simple-Parry Pisot 族 β_n，窗口 m=n-1 时映射单射而最优未来解码长度恰为 n-1，故三次内亦无统一界。
+现有核心工具如下：Parry admissibility、合法词 colex 排名的连续区间双射、Q_m 的 Parry–Bertrand 递推及二次两参数递推；
+差分字母表 [-d,d] 上的带状 Toeplitz 同余系统 T_{m,L}e≡0 mod Q_m，以及至多 (2d+1)^(m-1) 状态的 fiber-product 碰撞图；
+二次递推的相邻比值界、最近倍数分离 dist(beQ_{r-1},Q_r Z)≥Q_{r-2}、m 行有界滑动核消失及小窗口穷尽；
+显式三窗口解码器、双边分支轨道分类、近 Markov/AFT 临界正规形、Fischer-cover follower separation 与精确 Markov 阶证书；
+三次族的 Q_{j+1}>nQ_j、统一两窗口 carry 排除、共轭根误差界、终端碰撞路径 {0,±E_{n,r}} 的精确归纳分类；
+可计算证书包括有限图的环检测、最长路、周期碰撞见证及有限参数回归脚本；这些计算不是参数一致定理的证明。
+另有根单位滤子给出的纤维 Fourier 公式、仿射余类转移及二阶矩递推，但熵、KL、Blackwell 等结论只是共轭后的标准推论。
+以下路线已经尝试而失败，请勿原样再提：
+（1）把整数语言秩同余解释为 β-数值相等或 Z[β] 中的正规化：映射有明确反例不保持 β-值，故不存在该桥梁。
+（2）攻击 Schmidt 的 Salem–Parry 猜想：有限碰撞图须先有有限 Parry 数据，恰把待证周期性当前提，因而循环。
+（3）攻击 Hejda–Steiner 关于 γ(β) 的两个问题：γ(β) 由自然延拓域、β-tile 边界及共轭坐标极值控制，现有 Q_m 同余估计不提供这些几何量。
+（4）攻击 Hejda–Steiner 关于 β-adic 整数前缀结构的问题：Q_m 通常互不整除，现有余类没有已证 bonding maps，不能组成所需逆系统。
+（5）以受限系数多项式倍式的最小次数控制解码长度：simple-Parry 情形该次数退化为 Parry 阶，且 p-bonacci 与三次反例给出双向及无界分离。
+（6）断言负共轭二次族在所有 m≥4 的两输出解码完备：有限枚举不能穷尽非零常数 carry 分支，所谓完整有界-carry 分类尚未建立。
+（7）本轮冲刺已有两次高置信建议被后验验证否决：一次依赖的 phase boundary 经直接数值检验为假；另一次声称的 completeness classification 无法建立。
+可直接引用而无需重证：Rényi–Parry 的 greedy β-expansion/β-shift 与 admissibility；Schmidt 的 Pisot 最终周期性；
+Bertrand–Mathis、Bruyère–Hansel、Loraud、Lecomte–Rigo 及 Charlier–Cisternino–Stipulanti 的 Bertrand/抽象语言计数与可识别性；
+Frougny 等人的 Pisot 数值正规化有限自动机；Bassino 的三次 Pisot 展开分类；Lind–Marcus、Nasu、Fischer、Krieger 的 sliding code、fiber product 与 Fischer cover 理论；
+Zeckendorf/Ostrowski 的标准表示结论及 Drungilas 等人的受限系数多项式倍式结果；但不得把这些结论与本文自定义循环秩映射等同。
+请给出至多三项候选，按“成功概率 × 期刊层级影响”排序，并分别给出概率、预期 tier 及综合排序依据。
+每项必须写出可投稿的标准术语定理陈述；若为命名公开问题，须注明提出者、论文或专著、具体章节/页码/问题编号及可核验标识符。
+请逐项指出现有工具可直接承担的证明环节、仍缺的最小桥接引理、该引理为何并非把公开问题改写后重新假设，以及最短证明路线。
+尤其请区分“只加强本文自定义映射”与“解决标准 β-动力系统、数系、符号动力学或自动机理论中的既有问题”，不得以术语相似替代数学连接。
+对每项建议必须同时给出最便宜而有决定性的 falsification test：应计算、检索或证明什么，一旦出现何种结果即否决该路线。
+请标明您亲自核验过的等式、文献定位和有限例，以及任何尚未核验、仅属启发或依赖数据库完整性的步骤；不得以置信措辞掩盖未验证环节。
+若上述工具实际上达不到任何同层级命名公开问题，请明确回答“不可达到”，并说明结构性障碍及仍可诚实争取的最强内部定理；此结论同样具有高价值。

+其值为 3 当且仅当最小多项式为 x^2-ax-a（a≥1）或 x^2-ax+1（a≥3），其余情形为 2。
+阈值以上，长度 n≥m 的有限块映射单射，像语言复杂度为 (⌊β⌋+1)^(n+m-1)，逆码具有零记忆和有限预见。
+并已证有限块单射首次出现于 n=m，像在固定输出字母表中恰为 m-step 而非 (m-1)-step SFT，自然重叠图为右 Fischer cover。
+已证结果二：对任意非整数 simple-Parry 数（无需 Pisot 性），未来单侧解码延迟恰等于有限差分碰撞图中坏初态的最长存活路径。
+在此类中，双边单射等价于有限未来解码；不单射时存在周期不超过 (2d+1)^(m-1)-1 的碰撞证书，窗口 m=2 已完全三分。
+已证结果三：存在显式三次 simple-Parry Pisot 族 β_n，窗口 m=n-1 时映射单射而最优未来解码长度恰为 n-1，故三次内亦无统一界。
+现有核心工具如下：Parry admissibility、合法词 colex 排名的连续区间双射、Q_m 的 Parry–Bertrand 递推及二次两参数递推；
+差分字母表 [-d,d] 上的带状 Toeplitz 同余系统 T_{m,L}e≡0 mod Q_m，以及至多 (2d+1)^(m-1) 状态的 fiber-product 碰撞图；
+二次递推的相邻比值界、最近倍数分离 dist(beQ_{r-1},Q_r Z)≥Q_{r-2}、m 行有界滑动核消失及小窗口穷尽；
+显式三窗口解码器、双边分支轨道分类、近 Markov/AFT 临界正规形、Fischer-cover follower separation 与精确 Markov 阶证书；
+三次族的 Q_{j+1}>nQ_j、统一两窗口 carry 排除、共轭根误差界、终端碰撞路径 {0,±E_{n,r}} 的精确归纳分类；
+可计算证书包括有限图的环检测、最长路、周期碰撞见证及有限参数回归脚本；这些计算不是参数一致定理的证明。
+另有根单位滤子给出的纤维 Fourier 公式、仿射余类转移及二阶矩递推，但熵、KL、Blackwell 等结论只是共轭后的标准推论。
+以下路线已经尝试而失败，请勿原样再提：
+（1）把整数语言秩同余解释为 β-数值相等或 Z[β] 中的正规化：映射有明确反例不保持 β-值，故不存在该桥梁。
+（2）攻击 Schmidt 的 Salem–Parry 猜想：有限碰撞图须先有有限 Parry 数据，恰把待证周期性当前提，因而循环。
+（3）攻击 Hejda–Steiner 关于 γ(β) 的两个问题：γ(β) 由自然延拓域、β-tile 边界及共轭坐标极值控制，现有 Q_m 同余估计不提供这些几何量。
+（4）攻击 Hejda–Steiner 关于 β-adic 整数前缀结构的问题：Q_m 通常互不整除，现有余类没有已证 bonding maps，不能组成所需逆系统。
+（5）以受限系数多项式倍式的最小次数控制解码长度：simple-Parry 情形该次数退化为 Parry 阶，且 p-bonacci 与三次反例给出双向及无界分离。
+（6）断言负共轭二次族在所有 m≥4 的两输出解码完备：有限枚举不能穷尽非零常数 carry 分支，所谓完整有界-carry 分类尚未建立。
+（7）本轮冲刺已有两次高置信建议被后验验证否决：一次依赖的 phase boundary 经直接数值检验为假；另一次声称的 completeness classification 无法建立。
+可直接引用而无需重证：Rényi–Parry 的 greedy β-expansion/β-shift 与 admissibility；Schmidt 的 Pisot 最终周期性；
+Bertrand–Mathis、Bruyère–Hansel、Loraud、Lecomte–Rigo 及 Charlier–Cisternino–Stipulanti 的 Bertrand/抽象语言计数与可识别性；
+Frougny 等人的 Pisot 数值正规化有限自动机；Bassino 的三次 Pisot 展开分类；Lind–Marcus、Nasu、Fischer、Krieger 的 sliding code、fiber product 与 Fischer cover 理论；
+Zeckendorf/Ostrowski 的标准表示结论及 Drungilas 等人的受限系数多项式倍式结果；但不得把这些结论与本文自定义循环秩映射等同。
+请给出至多三项候选，按“成功概率 × 期刊层级影响”排序，并分别给出概率、预期 tier 及综合排序依据。
+每项必须写出可投稿的标准术语定理陈述；若为命名公开问题，须注明提出者、论文或专著、具体章节/页码/问题编号及可核验标识符。

+尤其请区分“只加强本文自定义映射”与“解决标准 β-动力系统、数系、符号动力学或自动机理论中的既有问题”，不得以术语相似替代数学连接。

---
name: verification-before-completion
description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always
---

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

Claiming work is complete without verification is dishonesty, not efficiency.

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence â‰  evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter â‰  compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion â‰  excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
âœ… [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
âŒ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
âœ… Write â†’ Run (pass) â†’ Revert fix â†’ Run (MUST FAIL) â†’ Restore â†’ Run (pass)
âŒ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
âœ… [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
âŒ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
âœ… Re-read plan â†’ Create checklist â†’ Verify each â†’ Report gaps or completion
âŒ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
âœ… Agent reports success â†’ Check VCS diff â†’ Verify changes â†’ Report actual state
âŒ Trust agent report
```

## Why This Matters

From 24 failure memories:
- your human partner said "I don't believe you" - trust broken
- Undefined functions shipped - would crash
- Missing requirements shipped - incomplete features
- Time wasted on false completion â†’ redirect â†’ rework
- Violates: "Honesty is a core value. If you lie, you'll be replaced."

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

## The Bottom Line

**No shortcuts for verification.**

Run the command. Read the output. THEN claim the result.

This is non-negotiable.

+其值为 3 当且仅当最小多项式为 x^2-ax-a（a≥1）或 x^2-ax+1（a≥3），其余情形为 2。
+阈值以上，长度 n≥m 的有限块映射单射，像语言复杂度为 (⌊β⌋+1)^(n+m-1)，逆码具有零记忆和有限预见。
+并已证有限块单射首次出现于 n=m，像在固定输出字母表中恰为 m-step 而非 (m-1)-step SFT，自然重叠图为右 Fischer cover。
+已证结果二：对任意非整数 simple-Parry 数（无需 Pisot 性），未来单侧解码延迟恰等于有限差分碰撞图中坏初态的最长存活路径。
+在此类中，双边单射等价于有限未来解码；不单射时存在周期不超过 (2d+1)^(m-1)-1 的碰撞证书，窗口 m=2 已完全三分。
+已证结果三：存在显式三次 simple-Parry Pisot 族 β_n，窗口 m=n-1 时映射单射而最优未来解码长度恰为 n-1，故三次内亦无统一界。
+现有核心工具如下：Parry admissibility、合法词 colex 排名的连续区间双射、Q_m 的 Parry–Bertrand 递推及二次两参数递推；
+差分字母表 [-d,d] 上的带状 Toeplitz 同余系统 T_{m,L}e≡0 mod Q_m，以及至多 (2d+1)^(m-1) 状态的 fiber-product 碰撞图；
+二次递推的相邻比值界、最近倍数分离 dist(beQ_{r-1},Q_r Z)≥Q_{r-2}、m 行有界滑动核消失及小窗口穷尽；
+显式三窗口解码器、双边分支轨道分类、近 Markov/AFT 临界正规形、Fischer-cover follower separation 与精确 Markov 阶证书；
+三次族的 Q_{j+1}>nQ_j、统一两窗口 carry 排除、共轭根误差界、终端碰撞路径 {0,±E_{n,r}} 的精确归纳分类；
+可计算证书包括有限图的环检测、最长路、周期碰撞见证及有限参数回归脚本；这些计算不是参数一致定理的证明。
+另有根单位滤子给出的纤维 Fourier 公式、仿射余类转移及二阶矩递推，但熵、KL、Blackwell 等结论只是共轭后的标准推论。
+以下路线已经尝试而失败，请勿原样再提：
+（1）把整数语言秩同余解释为 β-数值相等或 Z[β] 中的正规化：映射有明确反例不保持 β-值，故不存在该桥梁。
+（2）攻击 Schmidt 的 Salem–Parry 猜想：有限碰撞图须先有有限 Parry 数据，恰把待证周期性当前提，因而循环。
+（3）攻击 Hejda–Steiner 关于 γ(β) 的两个问题：γ(β) 由自然延拓域、β-tile 边界及共轭坐标极值控制，现有 Q_m 同余估计不提供这些几何量。
+（4）攻击 Hejda–Steiner 关于 β-adic 整数前缀结构的问题：Q_m 通常互不整除，现有余类没有已证 bonding maps，不能组成所需逆系统。
+（5）以受限系数多项式倍式的最小次数控制解码长度：simple-Parry 情形该次数退化为 Parry 阶，且 p-bonacci 与三次反例给出双向及无界分离。
+（6）断言负共轭二次族在所有 m≥4 的两输出解码完备：有限枚举不能穷尽非零常数 carry 分支，所谓完整有界-carry 分类尚未建立。
+（7）本轮冲刺已有两次高置信建议被后验验证否决：一次依赖的 phase boundary 经直接数值检验为假；另一次声称的 completeness classification 无法建立。
+可直接引用而无需重证：Rényi–Parry 的 greedy β-expansion/β-shift 与 admissibility；Schmidt 的 Pisot 最终周期性；
+Bertrand–Mathis、Bruyère–Hansel、Loraud、Lecomte–Rigo 及 Charlier–Cisternino–Stipulanti 的 Bertrand/抽象语言计数与可识别性；
+Frougny 等人的 Pisot 数值正规化有限自动机；Bassino 的三次 Pisot 展开分类；Lind–Marcus、Nasu、Fischer、Krieger 的 sliding code、fiber product 与 Fischer cover 理论；
+Zeckendorf/Ostrowski 的标准表示结论及 Drungilas 等人的受限系数多项式倍式结果；但不得把这些结论与本文自定义循环秩映射等同。
+请给出至多三项候选，按“成功概率 × 期刊层级影响”排序，并分别给出概率、预期 tier 及综合排序依据。
+每项必须写出可投稿的标准术语定理陈述；若为命名公开问题，须注明提出者、论文或专著、具体章节/页码/问题编号及可核验标识符。
+请逐项指出现有工具可直接承担的证明环节、仍缺的最小桥接引理、该引理为何并非把公开问题改写后重新假设，以及最短证明路线。
+尤其请区分“只加强本文自定义映射”与“解决标准 β-动力系统、数系、符号动力学或自动机理论中的既有问题”，不得以术语相似替代数学连接。
+对每项建议必须同时给出最便宜而有决定性的 falsification test：应计算、检索或证明什么，一旦出现何种结果即否决该路线。
+请标明您亲自核验过的等式、文献定位和有限例，以及任何尚未核验、仅属启发或依赖数据库完整性的步骤；不得以置信措辞掩盖未验证环节。
+若上述工具实际上达不到任何同层级命名公开问题，请明确回答“不可达到”，并说明结构性障碍及仍可诚实争取的最强内部定理；此结论同样具有高价值。
+建议若不能同时通过标准对象桥接、非循环性、参数一致性及自身证伪测试，即不应列入前三项。

+本文研究的不是数值 β-正规化，而是如下 sliding block code：对 greedy β-shift 的长度 m 语言作 colexicographic 整数排名，
+将同一权重和延拓到完整数字立方体，模合法词数 Q_m 取余，再以唯一合法词代表该余类，并沿输入序列重叠滑动。
